commutes with reflexive coequalisers.

Proof.

Part (i) follows from the naturality of the operators on
\TR
and the fact that
F
,
V
, and the action of the cyclic generator are maps of spectra, and the monoidality of
R
and
F
follows from the lax monoidality of
\TR
and
\TR_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}
.
Part (ii) follows from Proposition
2.8
. Parts (iii) and (iv) follow from Propositions
2.7
and
2.8
and the fact that
F
and
V
are respectively restriction and transfer of the
C_{p^{n}}
-Mackey functor
\underline{\pi}_{0}
of
\underline{\TR}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)
, and
\sigma_{i}
its Weyl action.

The first diagram in Part (v) commutes by definition of
\tau
for
\underline{\TR}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}
via the truncation
of
\tau\colon\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\underline{M}\to\underline{\TR}(R;M)
and by Proposition
2.8
. Let us show that the second diagram commutes. Since
\tau
is defined from a map of
C_{p^{n+1}}
-spaces, it is compatible with the Frobenius. Thus the diagram

\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\textstyle{\FF_{n+1}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{M^{\times p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{p}}
\textstyle{\FF_{n}(R;{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$})}

commutes, where
\tau_{p}
is the value at
C_{p^{n+1}}/C_{p^{n}}
of the map on
\pi_{0}
-coefficient systems induced by the morphism of
C_{p^{n+1}}
-spaces
\tau
.
It is then sufficient to show that
\tau_{p}
factors as the composite
M^{\times p}\to{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$}\to\FF_{n}(R;{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$})
of the canonical map and the map
\tau
for the bimodule
{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$}
(recall that we are assuming that
R
and
M
are discrete). By adjoining the infinite loop space and
\underline{\TR}
, this is the case if the diagram of polygonic spectra

\textstyle{\sh_{p}L(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\underline{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sh_{p}\underline{\THH}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\underline{{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\THH}(R;{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$})}

commutes, where the horizontal maps are the maps defining
\tau
under the adjunction, and the vertical map is induced by the canonical map
M^{\times p}\to{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$}
. This holds by definition of the horizontal maps.

The properties of
\FF_{1}(R;M)
follow from the fact that
\TR_{\langle 1\rangle}(R;M)=\THH(R;M)
(Example
2.4
) and the definition of the cyclic action on
\THH(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p})
.
Part (vi) follows from the fibre sequence involving the map
R
for
\TR_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)
.
Let us show Part (vii).
Given a reflexive coequaliser

\textstyle{(R_{1};M_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(R_{0};M_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(R;M)}

in the category
\bimod
of (discrete) bimodules, we need to show that

\textstyle{\FF_{n}(R_{1};M_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\FF_{n}(R_{0};M_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\FF_{n}(R;M)}

is a coequaliser of abelian groups. Using the right Kan extension, the diagram

\textstyle{(R_{1};M_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(R_{0};M_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

can be extended to a simplicial object
(R_{\bullet};M_{\bullet})
in
\bimod
(explicitly this can be done by taking iterated pullbacks which are computed underlying, as limits in
\bimod
are). Upon taking Eilenberg-MacLane spectra we obtain a simplicial object
(HR_{\bullet};HM_{\bullet})
in the category of bimodule spectra.
Since smash powers and smash products of spectra commute with sifted colimits,
\THH(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p^{k}})
commutes with sifted colimits of bimodule spectra for every
k\geq 0
. Thus by induction on the fibre sequences

\THH(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p^{k}})_{hC_{p^{k}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle V}}{{\longrightarrow}}\TR_{\langle p^{k}\rangle}(R;M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle R}}{{\longrightarrow}}\TR_{\langle p^{k-1}\rangle}(R;M)

so does
\TR_{\langle p^{k}\rangle}
for all
k\geq 0
. It follows that

|\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(HR_{\bullet};HM_{\bullet})|\simeq\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(|HR_{\bullet}|;|HM_{\bullet}|).

After applying
\pi_{0}
on both sides and using Corollary
2.10
we get an isomorphism

\pi_{0}|\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(HR_{\bullet};HM_{\bullet})|\cong\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(H\pi_{0}|HR_{\bullet}|;H\pi_{0}|HM_{\bullet}|)\cong\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(R;M)=\FF_{n}(R;M).

On the other hand
\pi_{0}|\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(HR_{\bullet};HM_{\bullet})|
fits into the coequaliser diagram

\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(HR_{1};HM_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(HR_{0};HM_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}|\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(HR_{\bullet};HM_{\bullet})|}

which proves the desired result.
∎

Remark 2.12
.

The proof of Theorem
2.5
that we give just below works for any collection of functors
\mathcal{F}_{n}
with operators which satisfy the conditions of Proposition
2.11
.

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

Let
(R;M)
be a bimodule. We start by defining a map

I_{n+1}\colon W_{n+1,p}(R;M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{F}_{n+1}(R;M)

by taking a representative
(m_{0},m_{1},\dots,m_{n})
of a class in
W_{n+1,p}(R;M)
to

I_{n+1}(m_{0},m_{1},\dots,m_{n}):=\sum_{i=0}^{n}V^{i}\tau^{n-i}(m_{i}),

where
\tau^{n-i}\colon M^{\otimes_{R}p^{i}}\to\mathcal{F}_{n+1-i}(R;M^{\otimes_{R}p^{i}})
is the map
\tau
for the bimodule
M^{\otimes_{R}p^{i}}
, and we wrote

V^{i}\colon\mathcal{F}_{n+1-i}(R;M^{\otimes_{R}p^{i}})\to\mathcal{F}_{n+1}(R;M)
for the iteration of the map
V
. In order to show that
I_{n+1}
is well-defined, and ultimately an isomorphism, we need to define an analogue of the ghost maps for
\FF_{n+1}(R;M)
. Define for
0\leq j<n+1
,

\overline{w}_{j}:=F^{j}R^{n-j}\colon\FF_{n+1}(R;M)\to\FF_{1}(R;{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{j}}$})^{C_{p^{j}}}\cong({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{j}}$}/[R,{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{j}}$}])^{C_{p^{j}}}=({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{j}}$})^{C_{p^{j}}}

where for the latter identification we used (v).
We want to verify that under the map
I_{n+1}
this map corresponds to the usual ghost map, i.e. that the diagram

\textstyle{\prod_{i=0}^{n}{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i}}$}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{w_{j}}
\textstyle{\FF_{n+1}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{w}_{j}}
\textstyle{({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{j}}$})^{C_{p^{j}}}}

commutes. To see this we first observe that using (iii)-(iv) the following identities hold:

\displaystyle F^{j}V^{j}

\displaystyle=F^{j-1}FVV^{j-1}=F^{j-1}(\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\sigma_{1}^{k})V^{j-1}=(\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\sigma_{j}^{k})F^{j-1}V^{j-1}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\sigma_{j}^{k})(\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\sigma_{j}^{kp})F^{j-2}V^{j-2}=\dots=(\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\sigma_{j}^{k})(\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\sigma_{j}^{kp})\cdots(\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\sigma_{j}^{kp^{j-1}})=\sum_{k=0}^{p^{j}-1}\sigma_{j}^{k}=\sum_{\sigma\in C_{p^{j}}}\sigma,

as endomorphisms of
\FF_{n+1}(R;{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{j}}$})
.
It then follows that

\displaystyle\overline{w}_{j}I_{{n+1}}(m_{0},\dots,m_{n})

\displaystyle=F^{j}R^{n-j}\sum_{i=0}^{n}V^{i}\tau^{n-i}(m_{i})

\displaystyle=F^{j}\sum_{i=0}^{j}V^{i}R^{n-j}\tau^{n-i}(m_{i}),

where we used (ii) and in particular that
R^{n-j}V^{i}=0
on
\FF_{n-i+1}(R;{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i}}$})
if
i>j
. Further using (v), (iii) and the previous paragraph, we get

\displaystyle F^{j}\sum_{i=0}^{j}V^{i}R^{n-j}\tau^{n-i}(m_{i})=\sum_{i=0}^{j}\sum_{\sigma\in C_{p^{j}}/C_{p^{j-i}}}\sigma F^{j-i}\tau^{j-i}(m_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{j}\sum_{\sigma\in C_{p^{j}}/C_{p^{j-i}}}\sigma m^{\otimes p^{j-i}}_{i}=w_{j}(m_{0},\dots,m_{n}),

which shows that the triangle above commutes.

Before showing that the map
I_{n+1}
descends to Witt vectors we check that the morphism

\overline{w}=(\overline{w}_{0},\dots,\overline{w}_{n})\colon\FF_{n+1}(R,M)\to\prod_{j=0}^{n}({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{j}}$})^{C_{p^{j}}}

is injective when
(R;M)
is free (or more generally when
\tr\colon({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{j}}$})_{C_{p^{j}}}\to({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{j}}$})^{C_{p^{j}}}
is injective, see
[
DKNP22
, Lemma 1.4 and Proposition 1.18]
). For
n=0
, the map
\overline{w}
is just the isomorphism

\FF_{1}(R;M)\cong M/[R,M].

Now inductively assume that

\overline{w}=(\overline{w}_{0},\dots,\overline{w}_{n-1})\colon\FF_{n}(R;M)\longrightarrow\prod_{j=0}^{n-1}({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{j}}$})^{C_{p^{j}}}

is injective. Consider the diagram with exact rows

\textstyle{({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{n}}$})_{C_{p^{n}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tr}
\scriptstyle{V^{n}}
\textstyle{\FF_{n+1}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{w}}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{\FF_{n}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{w}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{n}}$})^{C_{p^{n}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\prod_{j=0}^{n}({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{j}}$})^{C_{p^{j}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\proj}
\textstyle{\prod_{j=0}^{n-1}({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{j}}$})^{C_{p^{j}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

where the left hand diagram commutes because of (ii), (iv) and (v). The commutativity of the right hand diagram is clear. The left hand vertical map is injective since
(R;M)
is free. Assuming inductively that the right-hand map is also injective, the standard diagram chase shows that the middle map is injective as well.

For free
(R;M)
, since
\overline{w}
is injective, the definition of the relation defining
W_{n+1,p}(R;M)
shows that
I_{n+1}
descends to a well-defined group homomorphism

I_{n+1}\colon W_{n+1,p}(R;M)\longrightarrow\FF_{n+1}(R;M)

which is moreover injective.
To see that it is also surjective, we consider the diagram

\textstyle{({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{n}}$})_{C_{p^{n}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{n}}
\textstyle{W_{n+1,p}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{W_{n,p}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{n}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{n}}$})_{C_{p^{n}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{n}}
\textstyle{\FF_{n+1}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{\FF_{n}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

The right hand square commutes by the construction of
I_{n}
and from the fact that
R
commutes with
V
and
\tau
. The left hand square commutes by the description of
V^{n}\colon({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{n}}$})_{C_{p^{n}}}\to W_{n+1,p}(R;M)
of Section
1.2
. The exactness of the top row follows from the results of
[
DKNP22
]
(see Section
1.2
). Now
I_{0}
is surjective by (v), and by induction so is the middle map by the standard diagram chase.

We have thus constructed a natural isomorphism

I_{n+1}\colon W_{n+1,p}(R;M)\cong\FF_{n+1}(R;M)

for any free bimodule
(R;M)
. Using the fact that both the source and target commute with reflexive coequalisers, this uniquely extends to a natural isomorphism on the whole category
\bimod
. Also using the naturality one can see that the formula for the general
I_{n}
is given as claimed above.

Finally, let us show that
I_{n+1}
is monoidal. Again since
\FF_{n+1}
and
W_{{n+1},p}
commute with reflexive coequalisers we may show this on the full subcategory of free bimodules. It is then sufficient to see that

\overline{w}_{j}(I_{n+1}(a)\ast I_{n+1}(b))=\overline{w}_{j}I_{n+1}(a\ast b)

holds for all
0\leq j\leq n
. By definition
\overline{w}_{j}=F^{j}R^{n-1-j}
and therefore it is monoidal by
i)
, and

\displaystyle\overline{w}_{j}(I_{n+1}(a)\ast I_{n+1}(b))

\displaystyle=\overline{w}_{j}(I_{n+1}(a))\ast\overline{w}_{j}(I_{n+1}(a))=w_{j}(a)\ast w_{j}(b)

\displaystyle=w_{j}(a\ast b)=\overline{w}_{j}I_{n-1}(a\ast b).

∎

2.2
The Mackey structure on the components of TR

We now identify the operators of
\TR
and the Witt vectors under the isomorphism of Theorem
2.5
. By Proposition
2.7
there is a genuine
C_{p^{n}}
-spectrum
\underline{\TR}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)
whose genuine
C_{p^{k}}
-fixed-points are the spectra
\TR_{\langle p^{k}\rangle}(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p^{n-k}})
, and in particular the next result identifies the Mackey structure of its components.

Proposition 2.13
.

Let
R
be a connective ring spectrum and
M
a connective
R
-bimodule. For any
n\geq 1
, the following diagrams commute:

\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;{\pi_{0}M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{528551}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{586095}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.74998pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\scriptstyle{I_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;\pi_{0}M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M),}

\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;\pi_{0}M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{I_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;\pi_{0}M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(R;M)}

\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;\pi_{0}M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{I_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;{\pi_{0}M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{528551}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{586095}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.74998pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p}),}

\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;{\pi_{0}M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{528551}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{586095}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.74998pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i}}$})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{i}}
\scriptstyle{I_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p^{i}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{i}}
\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;{\pi_{0}M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{528551}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{586095}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.74998pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i}}$})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p^{i}})}

\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;\pi_{0}M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M),}

In particular the maps
I_{k}
, for
1\leq k\leq n+1
, determine a monoidal isomorphism of
C_{p^{n}}
-Mackey functors between
\pi_{0}\underline{\TR}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)
and the Mackey functor
C_{p^{k}}\mapsto W_{\langle p^{k}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;{\pi_{0}M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{528551}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{586095}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.74998pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{n-k}}$})
equipped with the restriction maps
F
and the transfers
V
of §
1.2
.

Proof.

By Corollary
2.10
we can assume that
(R;M)
is discrete, and we prove this theorem for
W_{n,p}(R;M)
. Our argument will moreover work for any collection of functors
\FF_{n}
satisfying Proposition
2.11
.

The commutativity of the first diagram follows from the construction of the map
I_{n}
. Indeed, it suffices to show that the diagram commutes after precomposing with the projection

\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}{({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$})}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i}}$}\to W_{n,p}(R;{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$}).

We then compute:

\displaystyle VI_{n}(m_{0},\dots m_{n-1})=V\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}V^{i}\tau^{n-1-i}(m_{i})=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}V^{i+1}\tau^{n-1-i}(m_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{i}\tau^{n-1-{(i-1)}}(m_{i-1})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{i}\tau^{n-i}(m_{i-1})=I_{n+1}(0,m_{0},\dots,m_{n-1})=I_{n+1}V(m_{0},\dots,m_{n-1}).

Here we use the description of
V
on representatives as in Section
1.2
(see also
[
DKNP22
, Proposition 1.23]
), the identification

{({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$})}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i}}$}\cong{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i+1}}$}

and that under these identifications

\tau^{n-1-i}=\tau^{n-(i+1)}\colon{({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$})}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i}}$}={M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i+1}}$}\to\FF_{n-1-i+1}(R;{({M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$})}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i}}$})=\FF_{n-i}(R;{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i+1}}$}),

which follows from the naturality of
\tau
.

Next we check
RI_{n+1}=I_{n}R
. Again it suffices to check that the identity holds after precomposing with the projection

\prod_{i=0}^{n}{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{i}}$}\to W_{n+1,p}(R;M),

and we get that

\displaystyle RI_{n+1}(m_{0},\dots,m_{n})=R(\sum_{i=0}^{n}V^{i}\tau^{n-i}(m_{i}))=\sum_{i=0}^{n}RV^{i}\tau^{n-i}(m_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}V^{i}\tau^{n-1-i}(m_{i})=I_{n}R(m_{0},\dots m_{n}).

Here we have used the description of
R\colon W_{n+1,p}(R;M)\to W_{n,p}(R;M)
as in Section
1.2
, as well as (ii) and (v). In particular, we have used
RV^{n}(m_{n})=0
.

For checking the commutativity of the next two diagrams we employ the ghost components. We first check that

\overline{w}FI_{n+1}=\overline{w}I_{n}F

in
\prod_{j=0}^{n-1}{M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{296813}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle R$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{220684}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle R$}}}}}}{\circledcirc}p^{j}}$}
. This implies the result when
(R;M)
is free since in this case
\overline{w}
is injective as observed above. For general
(R;M)
the result then follows by naturality. We verify the latter identity for every component
0\leq j\leq n-1
:

\overline{w}_{j}I_{n}F=w_{j}F=w_{j+1}=\overline{w}_{j+1}I_{n+1}=F^{j+1}R^{n-j-1}I_{n+1}=F^{j}R^{n-j-1}FI_{n+1}=\overline{w}_{j}FI_{n+1},

where we have used (ii) and
[
DKNP22
, Proposition 1.25]
.

For the fourth diagram, we check that the identity
\sigma_{i}I_{n}=I_{n}\sigma_{i}
holds. We can again assume that
(R;M)
is free and compute componentwise that for
0\leq j\leq n-1
:

\displaystyle\overline{w}_{j}I_{n}\sigma_{i}=w_{j}\sigma_{i}=\sigma_{i+j}w_{j}=\sigma_{i+j}\overline{w}_{j}I_{n}=\overline{w}_{j}\sigma_{i}I_{n},

where we used (iii).

The compatibility with
\tau
is immediate from the definition of
I_{n}
.

In order to identify the full Mackey structure simply notice that for every
0\leq k\leq n
there is an equivalence of
C_{p^{k}}
-equivariant spectra

\Res^{C_{p^{n}}}_{C_{p^{k}}}(\underline{\TR}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M))\simeq\underline{\TR}_{\langle p^{k}\rangle}(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p^{n-k}}).

It follows that the transfer of
\underline{\TR}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)
from
C_{p^{k-1}}
to
C_{p^{k}}
agrees with the same transfer for
\underline{\TR}_{\langle p^{k}\rangle}(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p^{n-k}})
, which by the first part of Proposition
2.13
applied to the bimodule
M^{\wedge_{R}p^{n-k}}
agrees with the Witt vectors Verschiebung. A similar argument identifies the lower restrictions and the Weyl actions.
∎

Remark 2.14
.

As in Remark
2.12
, the proof of Proposition
2.13
works for any collection of functors
\mathcal{F}_{n}
with operators which satisfy the conditions of Proposition
2.11
. In fact, the isomorphisms
I_{n}
are the unique isomorphisms which satisfy
2.13
.

Remark 2.15
.

Suppose that
R
is a connective
E_{\infty}
-ring spectrum and
M
a connective
E_{\infty}
-
R
-algebra. In this case we expect that analogously to the map
\tau
(Section
2.1
), one can construct multiplicative maps

N\colon\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p})\longrightarrow\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)

for every
n\geq 1
which endow
\pi_{0}\underline{\TR}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)
with the structure of a
C_{p^{n}}
-Tambara functor.
These maps should be characterised algebraically by the commutative diagram

\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;{\pi_{0}M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{528551}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{586095}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.74998pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p}$})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{N}
\scriptstyle{I_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n-1}\rangle}(R;M^{\wedge_{R}p})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{N}
\textstyle{W_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;\pi_{0}M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\TR_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)}

where the map
N
on the left is the norm operator of
1.19
. In particular the maps
I_{k}
, for
1\leq k\leq n+1
, should determine an isomorphism of
C_{p^{n}}
-Tambara functors between
\pi_{0}\underline{\TR}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(R;M)
and the Tambara functor
C_{p^{k}}\mapsto W_{\langle p^{k}\rangle}(\pi_{0}R;{\pi_{0}M}\lower 2.58334pt\hbox{${}^{\underset{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\displaystyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{665423}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.75pt}{$\textstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.65852pt}{\resizebox{528551}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.525pt}{$\scriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.31705pt}{\resizebox{586095}{196608}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.74998pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pi_{0}R$}}}}}}{\otimes}p^{n-k}}$})
equipped with the restriction maps
F
and the transfers
V
and the norm
N
of §
1.2
.

We leave these observations about the norms on
\TR
as open questions and encourage interested readers to work out the details.

2.3
Free Tambara functors and Witt vectors

In this section we describe the free
C_{p^{n}}
-Tambara functor in terms of Witt vectors with coefficients. This is a result analogous to Brun’s
[
Bru05
, Theorem B]
which establishes a relationship between these Tambara functors and the usual Witt vectors of a commutative ring.

We recall that that a
C_{p^{n}}
-Tambara functor
T
consists of a commutative ring
T(C_{p^{k}})
with a
C_{p^{n-k}}
-action for every
0\leq k\leq n
, together with equivariant maps

\textstyle{T(C_{p^{k+1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{T(C_{p^{k}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\scriptstyle{N}

for all
0\leq k\leq n-1
, where
F
is a ring homomorphism,
V
is additive, and
R
is multiplicative. These satisfy certain relations, which can be encoded by declaring
T
to be a finite products preserving functor on a certain category of double-spans (see
[
Tam93
]
).
We already saw in Remark
1.20
that for every commutative ring
A
, the functor
\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;A)
that sends
C_{p^{k}}
to the commutative ring

\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;A)(C_{p^{k}}):=W_{\langle p^{k}\rangle}(\Z;A^{\otimes p^{n-k}})

and equipped with the operators of §
1.2
is a
C_{p^{n}}
-Tambara functor.
Let
U
be the forgetful functor that takes a
C_{p^{n}}
-Tambara functor
T
to the underlying commutative ring
T(1)
.

Proposition 2.16
.

The functor that takes a commutative ring
A
to
\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;A)
is left adjoint to
U
.

Proof.

Let us start by defining a natural transformation

\hom_{C_{p^{n}}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Tamb}}(\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;A),T)\longrightarrow\hom_{\mathrm{Ring}}(A,T(1))

from the morphism set in the category of Tambara functors to that of the category of commutative rings.
We send a morphism of Tambara functors
\alpha\colon\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;A)\to T
to the ring homomorphism

f\colon A\xrightarrow{\id\otimes 1\otimes\dots\otimes 1}A^{\otimes p^{n}}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{0}}T(1)

where
\alpha_{0}
is the value at the trivial group of the natural transformation
\alpha
. We notice that by assumption
\alpha_{0}
is
C_{p^{n}}
-equivariant, and therefore
f
does not depend on the choice of ordering made in the first map. Moreover
\alpha_{0}
can be recovered by
f
, since by equivariancy

\alpha_{0}(a_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes a_{p^{n}})=\alpha_{0}(a_{1}\otimes 1\otimes\dots\otimes 1)\cdot\dots\cdot\alpha_{0}(1\otimes\dots\otimes 1\otimes a_{p^{n}})=\prod_{l=1}^{p^{n}}\sigma_{p^{n}}^{l}f(a_{l})

where
\sigma_{p^{n}}\in C_{p^{n}}
is a generator
(in other words,
A^{\otimes p^{n}}
is the free commutative
C_{p^{n}}
-ring on the commutative ring
A
).

In order to see that the map above defined on hom sets is a bijection, we first assume that
A=\Z[X]
is the polynomial ring on a set
X
. We can then define an inverse as follows. Given a ring homomorphism
f\colon\Z[X]\to T(1)
, we define
\alpha_{0}\colon\Z[X]^{\otimes p^{n}}\to T(1)
by the formula above. In order to define
\alpha_{C_{p^{k}}}
for
0<k\leq n
we recall from Proposition
1.14
that the group
W_{\langle p^{k}\rangle}(\Z;\Z[X]^{\otimes p^{n-k}})
is free abelian, generated by the elements
V^{i}\tau^{k-i}(m_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes m_{p^{i}})
, where
0\leq i\leq k
, and
(m_{1},\dots,m_{p^{i}})
ranges through the orbits of the
C_{p^{i}}
-action on the
p^{i}
-fold product of additive generators of the free abelian group
\Z[X]^{\otimes p^{n-k}}
, that is on the monomials
m_{l}
in the set
X^{\amalg p^{n-k}}
. Since
\tau
relates to
N
by Proposition
1.21
and
\alpha
needs to be compatible with the Tambara structure, we must define
\alpha_{C_{p^{k}}}
by

\alpha_{C_{p^{k}}}(V^{i}\tau^{k-i}(m_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes m_{p^{i}}))=V^{i}N^{k-i}\alpha_{0}((m_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes m_{p^{i}})\otimes 1^{\otimes(p^{k-i}-1)})

where
V
and
N
on the right hand side are the transfer and norm maps of
T
, and
1
is the unit of
\Z[X]^{\otimes p^{n-k+i}}
. Since these are free generators, this gives a well-defined additive map

\alpha_{C_{p^{k}}}\colon W_{\langle p^{k}\rangle}(\Z;\Z[X]^{\otimes p^{n-k}})\longrightarrow T(C_{p^{k}}),

for every
0\leq k\leq n
. This map is moreover multiplicative, since by the formula of Proposition
1.14
,
\alpha_{C_{p^{k}}}
sends the product of two generators, with
i\leq j
, to (abusing notation below, we will denote the unit of some tensor powers of
A
just by
1
)

\displaystyle\alpha_{C_{p^{k}}}(V^{i}\tau^{k-i}(\otimes_{l=1}^{p^{i}}u_{l})\cdot V^{j}\tau^{k-j}(\otimes_{h=1}^{p^{j}}v_{h}))=\alpha_{C_{p^{k}}}(\sum_{\sigma\in C_{p^{i}}}V^{j}(\tau^{k-j}((\sigma(\otimes_{l=1}^{p^{i}}u_{l}))^{\otimes p^{j-i}}\cdot(\otimes_{h=1}^{p^{j}}v_{h}))))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in C_{p^{i}}}V^{j}N^{k-j}\alpha_{0}\big(((\sigma(\otimes_{l=1}^{p^{i}}u_{l}))^{\otimes p^{j-i}}\cdot(\otimes_{h=1}^{p^{j}}v_{h}))\otimes 1^{\otimes(p^{k-j}-1)}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in C_{p^{k}}/C_{p^{k-i}}}V^{j}N^{k-j}\alpha_{0}\big(\prod_{\omega\in C_{p^{k-i}}/C_{p^{k-j}}}\omega\sigma((u_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes u_{p^{i}})\otimes 1^{\otimes(p^{k-i}-1)})\cdot((v_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes v_{p^{j}})\otimes 1^{\otimes(p^{k-j}-1)})\big)

\displaystyle=V^{i}N^{k-i}\alpha_{0}((u_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes u_{p^{i}})\otimes 1^{\otimes(p^{k-i}-1)})\cdot V^{j}N^{k-j}\alpha_{0}((v_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes v_{p^{j}})\otimes 1^{\otimes(p^{k-j}-1)})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{C_{p^{k}}}(V^{i}\tau^{k-i}(\otimes_{l=1}^{p^{i}}u_{l}))\cdot\alpha_{C_{p^{k}}}(V^{i}\tau^{k-j}(\otimes_{h=1}^{p^{j}}v_{h})).

The fourth equality holds by an argument analogous to the calculation of the multiplicative structure of Proposition
1.14
, by using the Mackey and Tambara identities of
T
.

Let us now show that the collection of maps
\alpha
is compatible with the Tambara structures. The map
\alpha
commutes with the transfer maps, since these are additive and on generators

\displaystyle V\alpha_{C_{p^{k}}}(V^{i}\tau^{k-i}(m_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes m_{p^{i}}))

\displaystyle=VV^{i}N^{k-i}\alpha_{0}((m_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes m_{p^{i}})\otimes 1^{\otimes(p^{k-i}-1)})

\displaystyle=V^{i+1}N^{k+1-(i+1)}\alpha_{0}((m_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes m_{p^{i}}\otimes 1^{\otimes(p^{k+1-(i+1)}-1)})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{C_{p^{k+1}}}(V^{i+1}\tau^{k+1-(i+1)}(m_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes m_{p^{i}}))

\displaystyle=\alpha_{C_{p^{k+1}}}(VV^{i}\tau^{k-i}(m_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes m_{p^{i}}))

A similar argument shows that
\alpha
commutes with the restriction maps
F
on generators, and therefore on all elements since
F
is additive. Finally let us show that
\alpha
is compatible with the norms. The norm of a sum of elements is the sum of the norms of those elements plus a sum of transfer terms, see e.g.
[
Ang15
, Lemma 5.2]
for a precise formula. Since We already showed that
\alpha
is compatible with transfers and multiplication, it is sufficient to show that it commutes with the norms on additive generators. By
[
HM19
, Corollary 2.9]
, for abelian
p
-groups norm of a transfer can be described as a transfer applied to a specific polynomial only depending on the group under the consideration. Since we know that
\alpha
is multiplicative and commutes with transfers and Weyl actions, this shows that
\alpha
also commutes with norms.

This shows that
\alpha
is a well-defined map of Tambara functors. Using again that the Witt vectors above are free as abelian groups, one can easily see that the map that sends
f
to
\alpha
is an inverse for the map above, showing that the Witt vectors are a left adjoint on the subcategory of free commutative rings.

Now assume that
A
is any commutative ring, and consider the functorial reflexive coequaliser diagram of bimodules

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.75005pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.75005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\Z;\Z[\Z[A]])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.75005pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.75005pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.75005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\Z;\Z[A])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.58345pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-1.99997pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 116.58345pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\Z;A)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Since the category of Tambara functors is a category of product-preserving functors, and reflexive coequalisers of product-preserving functors are computed pointwise, the diagram

\textstyle{\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;\Z[\Z[A]])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;\Z[A])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;A)}

is a coequaliser in the category of
C_{p^{n}}
-Tambara functors. Thus for every
C_{p^{n}}
-Tambara functor
T
, there is a bijection

\hom_{C_{p^{n}}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Tamb}}(\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;A),T)\cong\hom_{C_{p^{n}}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Tamb}}\big(\colim(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.90211pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-37.90211pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;\Z[\Z[A]])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.90211pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.90211pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 47.90211pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;\Z[A]))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,T\big)

\cong\lim\big(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 75.40675pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-75.40675pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\hom_{C_{p^{n}}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Tamb}}(\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;\Z[\Z[A]]),T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.40675pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.40675pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 85.40675pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\hom_{C_{p^{n}}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Tamb}}(\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;\Z[A]),T)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\big)

\cong\lim\big(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 51.62512pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-51.62512pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\hom_{\mathrm{Ring}}(\Z[\Z[A]],T(0))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.62512pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.62512pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 61.62512pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\hom_{\mathrm{Ring}}(\Z[A],T(0))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\big)

\cong\hom_{\mathrm{Ring}}\big(\colim(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 18.41669pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.41669pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Z[\Z[A]]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.41669pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.41669pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 28.41669pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Z[A]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces),T(0)\big)\cong\hom_{\mathrm{Ring}}(A,T(0))

where the third isomorphism follows from the free case above. It is moreover not difficult to see that this bijection coincides with the natural transformation defined above.
∎

Remark 2.17
.

It is also possible to prove Proposition
2.16
from the results of
[
Ull13
]
, by identifying
\underline{W}_{\langle p^{n}\rangle}(\Z;A)
with the components of the norm via
2.15
, and use that
N^{C_{p^{n}}}_{e}
is the left adjoint of the forgetful functor from genuine
C_{p^{n}}
-commutative equivariant ring spectra to
E_{\infty}
-ring spectra.

Example 2.18
.

Let us describe explicitly the free
C_{p}
-Tambara functor on a commutative ring
A
. This is the diagram

L(A)=\big(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 35.51643pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.18619pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
